# Experiment ledger

Every experiment ID ever issued, one row each, appended when the experiment
starts -- not when it succeeds. Rows are never deleted. See
[`METHODOLOGY.md`](METHODOLOGY.md) for what the IDs and statuses mean.

Managed by `experiments/experiment_ids.py`; edit the status with
`python experiments/experiment_ids.py status <ID> <status>`.

Candidate time series classification datasets for later experiments, grouped
by examples × features, are in [`DATASETS_USED.md`](DATASETS_USED.md).

## Experiment designs

Each experiment's design, registered before its runs started. What every
experiment of a hypothesis shares -- the claim, the arms, the measure -- is in
[`HYPOTHESES.md`](HYPOTHESES.md); what one experiment adds or changes is here.
Moved here from `HYPOTHESES.md` on 2026-10-07, the text unchanged but for links; the
versions in force when each experiment ran are in `HYPOTHESES.md` at its
pre-registration commit, which its report names.

### `H0-E01` -- H0 on five datasets

The design is [H0](HYPOTHESES.md#h0----the-bug-fixed-fastener-converges-faster-than-the-2020-code) itself: five datasets (crop_mapping, USPS, har,
BASEHOCK, TOX_171), their budgets and splits, and the 4-of-5-seeds criterion
are all written there.

### `H0-E02` -- H0 on every other dataset

*Added 2026-09-25, after H0-E01 had finished and been reported. See*
[Revisions](HYPOTHESES.md#revisions).

**Why.** H0-E01 was not supported, and its five datasets split. The fixed code
reached the common target sooner in at least 4 of 5 seeds on TOX_171 (5 of 5)
and USPS (4 of 5); the 2020 code did on BASEHOCK (4 of 5); crop mapping and har
went neither way (2 against 3 each). Five datasets cannot say which pattern is
the general one. This experiment asks the same question on every other dataset
on disk.

**Which datasets: all 31 others.** Every dataset `datasets.load` knows except
H0-E01's five. Taking all of them, rather than choosing, is what keeps a choice
made after seeing results from steering the outcome.

| group | datasets | budget per run | split |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Earth observation | eodata | 1,000 fits | EO split, as crop mapping in H0 |
| Earth observation, small | satimage (6,430 rows) | 1,000 fits | protocol 60/20/20; too few rows for the EO split |
| other tall sets | covertype, miniboone, higgs, volkert, gas_drift | 6,000 fits | 50,000-row cap, 60/20/20 |
| challenge sets | madelon, gina | 6,000 fits | protocol 60/20/20 |
| paper benchmarks | the other 22 of the 25 | 6,000 fits | protocol 60/20/20 |

The budget rule is H0's: Earth-observation sets get the paper's EO budget of
1,000 fits, every other set gets 6,000. Arms, seeds, the fixed-target measure
and the target itself are exactly as in H0.

**Primary analysis: datasets as the unit.** With 31 datasets the per-dataset
4-of-5 rule is no longer the useful test; the question is whether the datasets
lean one way. Per dataset and seed, the effect is log2 of the ratio of the fits
each arm needed to reach the common target, 2020 over fixed. It is positive when
the fixed code was faster, zero on a tie. Per dataset, the effect is the mean
of its 5 seeds.

**Falsification criterion.** Supported if the per-dataset effects are
positive on more datasets than negative **and** a two-sided Wilcoxon
signed-rank test over the per-dataset effects is significant at alpha = 0.05.
Reversed -- the fixes slowed convergence -- if the same holds with the signs
swapped. Otherwise not supported: no consistent difference is detected over
these datasets. Datasets with a zero effect drop out of the test, as the
Wilcoxon test requires, and their count is reported.

**Incomplete datasets.** A dataset whose runs fail, or have fewer than 5 seeds
for both arms when the experiment is stopped, is left out of the test and
listed. If more than 6 of the 31 are left out, the result is reported as
incomplete rather than as a verdict.

**Reported beside it, as secondary:** H0's per-dataset 4-of-5 counts for every
dataset; the effect per dataset group from the table above; the same test over
all 36 datasets, H0-E01's five included, labelled as including datasets seen
before this experiment was registered; and final test AUF (K = 10) per arm.

**How it runs.** Several datasets at once, so the whole experiment fits in a
weekend. Runs then compete for the CPU, so wall clock is not comparable
between arms or datasets. Fits, which decide the result, are not affected.
Every tiny paper benchmark carries the caveat of *What the small sets can
support*: on the smallest, validation is 10-20 rows, and ties are common.

**Relation to H0-E01.** H0-E01's verdict stands on its own criterion and is
reported first. This experiment is separate evidence about the same claim.
Neither result is read as overriding the other; if they disagree, both are
reported with the disagreement stated.

### `H0-E03` -- H0 repeated with the top genes kept

Registered 2026-09-30, after H0-E01 and H0-E02 were reported and before any of
this experiment's runs started.

**Why.** After H0-E02 the fixed code was changed to keep the top genes
(`include_top_genes=True`, Revisions, 2026-09-30), and the protocol budget was
doubled on the 11 datasets whose search had not converged. H0-E01 and H0-E02
tested a fixed arm that no longer exists. This experiment asks H0's question of
the current code.

**Arms.** `fastener`, now `include_top_genes=True, allow_self_mating=False`:
it differs from the 2020 code only in drawing the mating pool without
replacement, so no item is mated with itself. `fastener_2020` is unchanged.
The claim tested is therefore narrower than H0-E02's: that removing
self-mating alone makes FASTENER converge faster.

**Datasets: all 36 of H0-E01 and H0-E02**, with their splits. Budgets: the three
Earth-observation sets that had 1,000 fits keep 1,000 (crop_mapping, eodata
and satimage); every other dataset gets `protocol.fit_budget`, which is 12,000
fits on the 11 in `protocol.LONG_BUDGET_DATASETS` and 6,000 on the rest.
Seeds, the fixed-target measure and the target are exactly as in H0.

**Primary analysis and falsification criterion.** As in H0-E02, over all 36
datasets: the per-dataset effect is the mean over 5 seeds of log2(fits the
2020 arm needed / fits the fixed arm needed). Supported if the effects are
positive on more datasets than negative **and** a two-sided Wilcoxon
signed-rank test over them is significant at alpha = 0.05; reversed if the same
holds with the signs swapped; otherwise not supported. Zero effects drop out of
the test and are counted.

**Incomplete datasets.** As in H0-E02. If more than 7 of the 36 are left out
(the same one in five), the result is reported as incomplete.

**Reported beside it, as secondary:** H0's per-dataset 4-of-5 counts; the
effect per dataset group; final test AUF (K = 10) per arm; and, labelled as
comparisons across experiments and not as tests, each dataset's effect here
beside its effect in H0-E01 or H0-E02. On the 11 long-budget datasets the
budget differs from the earlier experiments, so their effects are not
comparable one to one.

**How it runs.** As H0-E02: several datasets at once, so wall clock is not
comparable between arms; fits are unaffected.

**Relation to H0-E01 and H0-E02.** Their verdicts stand, about the code they
tested. This one is about the current code.

### `H1-E01` -- H1 on eight datasets

The design is [H1](HYPOTHESES.md#h1----nsga-iis-weakness-at-small-k-is-an-initialisation-artifact)
itself: `nsga2` against `nsga2_sparse` on the eight datasets listed there, 5
seeds, `protocol.fit_budget` fits per run, and the 4-of-5-seeds criterion on
EOData and at least one other dataset.

**How it ran.** `run_queue.py` at `3e1ce0c`, one `run_sota.py` process per
dataset, arm and seed, ten at a time. Stopped by the PI after 13 minutes, with
the ten EOData runs started and none finished, to restart with a monitor that
shows every job at once. Failed; see its report.

### `H1-E02` -- H1 on eight datasets, restarted

Registered 2026-10-07, after `H1-E01` was stopped and before any of this
experiment's runs started. No result of `H1-E01` was read.

**Design.** Exactly `H1-E01`'s: the arms, datasets, seeds, budgets and
criterion in [H1](HYPOTHESES.md#h1----nsga-iis-weakness-at-small-k-is-an-initialisation-artifact).

**How it runs.** `run_queue.py`, one `run_sota.py --seed` process per dataset,
arm and seed -- 80 jobs -- at most ten at a time, slowest datasets first. The
jobs compete for the CPU, so wall clock is not comparable between arms; fits,
which the criterion is stated in, are not affected. A seed run alone gives the
same front as the same seed inside a five-seed run (checked on madelon before
the run), so the split changes no result. Watched with
`monitor.py --experiment H1-E02`.

### `H2H3-E01` -- H2 and H3 on 39 datasets

Registered 2026-10-09, after DEC-33 and before any of this experiment's runs
started. No result of a swap arm from this round exists. The budget table
below is filled in from the calibration, and committed, before the first run;
nothing else in this design changes after it.

**Design.** [H2](HYPOTHESES.md#h2----a-size-preserving-move-changes-the-front-at-all-guided-or-not)
and [H3](HYPOTHESES.md#h3----the-guided-swap-improves-fastener-at-small-k) as
revised on 2026-10-09 ([DEC-33](DECISIONS.md#dec-33)), run together, as the
two hypotheses share their stock arm:

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener` (stock), `fastener_swap_random`, `fastener_swap_guided`, all on FASTENER clone `b7e724d` with the fixed mating |
| datasets | 39: EOData at the 50,000-row cap, and the UCR/UEA sets of [`DATASETS_USED.md`](DATASETS_USED.md) except DuckDuckGeese ([DEC-34](DECISIONS.md#dec-34)) |
| seeds | 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 |
| budget | per dataset, the same for all three arms; see *Budgets* |
| primary metric | test AUF, K = 10 |
| H2 | per dataset, mean over seeds of AUF(`fastener_swap_random`) minus AUF(`fastener`); supported if positive on more datasets than negative and a two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank test over them gives p < 0.05; reversed if the same with signs swapped |
| H3 | the same with `fastener_swap_guided`; the guidance is credited only if the guided arm also beats the random-swap arm by the same test (secondary) |
| incomplete | a dataset with fewer than 5 seeds for any arm it is read on is left out; more than 6 of 39 left out makes the reading incomplete |
| read by | `make_report.py` (`DATASET_UNIT`), H2 first, then H3 |

**Budgets.** Set by the rule in H2, *Fit budget*, from the calibration queue
`h2h3-calibration`: one Boruta run and one 12,000-fit stock FASTENER run per
dataset, seed 1999, validation scores only, started 2026-10-09 at `9fa04b7`
(jobs started after DEC-34 record `f7f475c` or `c562490`, whose search code is
the same) and read by `calibrate_budget.py`. Its run IDs are in
`results/h2h3-calibration/budgets.json`. Calibration runs are not runs of this
experiment and are not listed in its ledger row.

The calibration was ended early by the PI ([DEC-35](DECISIONS.md#dec-35)):
35 FASTENER runs had reached 12,000 fits; eodata, EigenWorms, MotorImagery and
PEMS-SF were stopped at 9,009, 8,439, 5,770 and 6,325 and are read to where
they stopped. Validation AUF (K = 10) at each grid value, Boruta's, and the
budget the rule gives; "settled at" is the first grid value meeting both
conditions. Seconds per fit are wall clock in the calibration, ten jobs
sharing the CPU, and include FASTENER's own bookkeeping.

**10 datasets at 2,000 fits, 2 at 4,000, 10 at 6,000, 17 at 12,000** (12 of
these because the curve was still rising after 6,000). FASTENER's validation
AUF was above Boruta's on every dataset by 1,000 fits, so condition (ii)
decided no budget. Read by `calibrate_budget.py` at the commit that adds this
table; the full table is `results/h2h3-calibration/budgets.csv`.

**How it runs.** `run_queue.py --experiment H2H3-E01`, one `run_sota.py --seed`
process per dataset, arm and seed -- 585 jobs -- at most ten at a time,
slowest datasets first, each job given its own dataset's budget. The jobs
compete for the CPU, so wall clock is not comparable between arms; fits, which
the budget and the criterion are stated in, are not affected. Watched with
`monitor.py --experiment H2H3-E01`.

**Expected cost.** Budget x seconds per fit x 15 runs per dataset, summed:
about 114 CPU-hours over 585 runs, so at least 11 hours of wall
clock at ten jobs at a time, more as the last long jobs run alone. EigenWorms
(about 1.8 h per run) and MotorImagery (1.6 h) are half of it. The swap arms
spend the same budget in fits; the time per fit is taken from stock FASTENER
and is assumed for them.

### `H4-E01` -- H4 on the 25 paper benchmarks, at equal compute

Registered 2026-10-10, after DEC-36 and DEC-37 and before any of this
experiment's runs started. No H4 result exists.

**Design.** [H4](HYPOTHESES.md#h4----the-published-fastener--fs-sds-ordering-does-not-survive-a-clean-protocol)
as revised on 2026-10-10:

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener` (stock, FASTENER clone `b7e724d`, fixed mating) and `fs_sds`, both driven by the protocol decision tree |
| datasets | the 25 paper benchmarks |
| seeds | 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 |
| budget | per dataset, the same process CPU time for both arms (*Budgets*); fits not capped; FS-SDS one twentieth per k over k = 1..20 |
| threads | one per numeric library ([DEC-37](DECISIONS.md#dec-37)) |
| primary metric | test AUF, K = 20 |
| criterion | per dataset, each arm's mean AUF over the 5 seeds; supported if FS-SDS is higher on more datasets and a two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank test over the 25 differences favours it at alpha = 0.05; refuted if the test favours FASTENER; otherwise the methods are comparable |
| secondary | mean absolute difference from the paper's Table 3, the paper's ordering checked dataset by dataset; each arm's fits, CPU and wall time per dataset |
| read by | `make_report.py` (`PAIRWISE["H4"]`) |

**Budgets.** From the calibration queue `h4-calibration`: one stock FASTENER
run per dataset at the protocol fit budget, seed 1999, single-threaded,
started 2026-10-10 12:26 UTC at `8631f14`, read by `cpu_budget.py`. The budget is its
search CPU time rounded up to 10 s. A first calibration with the default
thread pools is kept as `results/h4-calibration-multithreaded` and not used
(DEC-37). Five jobs of the repeat were first linked to that calibration's
folders, which share their names; the link was corrected from the run
folders' timestamps before the budgets were read, and `run_queue.py` now
matches only folders made after a job started.

**How it runs.** `run_queue.py --experiment H4-E01 --budgets
results/h4-calibration/budgets.json`, one `run_sota.py --seed` process per
dataset, arm and seed -- 250 jobs -- at most ten at a time, longest budgets
first. Each process is single-threaded, so ten share the machine without
changing each other's CPU time. Watched with `monitor.py --experiment H4-E01`.

**Expected cost.** Budget x 10 runs per dataset: 8,010 x 10 = 80,100 CPU s,
about 22 CPU-hours, so about 2.2 to 3 hours of wall clock at ten
at a time. The longest run is CLL_SUB_111's, 720 s.

### `H4-E02` -- H4 on the 25 paper benchmarks with the ELM, at equal compute

Registered 2026-10-10, after H4-E01 was reported and before any of this
experiment's runs started.

**Design.** Exactly H4-E01's -- datasets, seeds, CPU budgets, single thread,
metric (test AUF, K = 20) and criterion -- except the classifier
([DEC-38](DECISIONS.md#dec-38)):

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener_elm` (stock FASTENER, clone `b7e724d`, fixed mating) and `fs_sds_elm`, both driven **and scored** by the ELM |
| ELM | `paper_protocol.ELMClassifier`: 160 sigmoid hidden neurons, input weights and biases uniform in [-1, 1] from seed 20200, inputs standardised on the training rows, output layer by least squares |
| budget | H4-E01's CPU budgets, `results/h4-calibration/budgets.json`, the table in H4-E01's design; FS-SDS one twentieth per k |
| criterion | H4's: supported if FS-SDS-ELM is higher on more datasets and a two-sided Wilcoxon over the 25 favours it at alpha = 0.05; refuted if it favours FASTENER-ELM; otherwise comparable |
| secondary | the 2 x 2 with H4-E01: per method, ELM minus tree; per classifier, FS-SDS minus FASTENER (counts and Wilcoxon, descriptive); `fs_sds_elm` against the paper's published FS-SDS row; fits, CPU and wall time per arm |

**How it runs.** `run_queue.py --experiment H4-E02 --methods
fs_sds,fastener:none --budgets results/h4-calibration/budgets.json --
--search-model elm`: 250 jobs, ten at a time, longest budgets first. Watched
with `monitor.py --experiment H4-E02`.

**Expected cost.** The same CPU budgets as H4-E01: about 22 CPU-hours, so
about 2.5 hours at ten at a time.

### `H4-E03` -- H4 on the 25 paper benchmarks, at equal model fits

Registered 2026-10-11, after H4-E01 and H4-E02 were reported and before any
of this experiment's runs started.

**Design.** H4 as registered, with FASTENER's bookkeeping vectorised
([DEC-39](DECISIONS.md#dec-39)):

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener` (stock FASTENER, clone `3877a1a`, fixed mating) and `fs_sds`, both driven and scored by the protocol decision tree |
| datasets | the 25 paper benchmarks |
| seeds | 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024 |
| budget | equal model fits per run: `protocol.fit_budget` -- 6,000, or 12,000 on arcene, BASEHOCK, CLL_SUB_111, lung_discrete, nci9, ORL, PCMAC, RELATHE, TOX_171, warpAR10P and warpPIE10P; FS-SDS 300 (600) per k over k = 1..20 |
| threads | one per numeric library (DEC-37) |
| primary metric | test AUF, K = 20 |
| criterion | H4's: supported if FS-SDS is higher on more datasets and a two-sided Wilcoxon over the 25 favours it at alpha = 0.05; refuted if it favours FASTENER; otherwise comparable |
| secondary | the paper's Table 3; fits, CPU and wall time per arm; a comparison with H4-E01 (equal CPU time), descriptive |

**How it runs.** `run_queue.py --experiment H4-E03 --methods
fs_sds,fastener:none`, no budget file: each run gets the protocol fit budget.
250 jobs, ten at a time, slowest datasets first. Watched with
`monitor.py --experiment H4-E03`.

**Expected cost.** FASTENER's time at these budgets is about the calibration's
(25 datasets, 8,010 CPU s per seed before the speed-up); FS-SDS at the same
fits takes less. About 10-14 CPU-hours, so roughly 1.5-2 hours at ten at a
time.

### `H5H6-E01` -- H5 and H6, the full grid on the C1-C7 datasets and EOData

Registered 2026-10-12, after a timing pilot and before any of this
experiment's runs started.

**Design.** H5 and H6 as revised on 2026-10-12 ([DEC-40](DECISIONS.md#dec-40)):

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener`, `fastener_swap_random`, `fastener_swap_guided` (clone `3be5697`, fixed mating); `nsga2`, `nsga2_sparse`, `nsgaii_miip`, `poss`, `fs_sds`; `boruta`, `random`; `mrmr`, `relieff`, `hsic_lasso`, `kbest_mutual_info`, `kbest_anova_f`, `tree_importance`, `rfe_tree`; `all_features` (reference only) -- all driven and scored by the protocol decision tree |
| datasets | H2/H3's 39: 38 UCR/UEA sets from clusters C1-C7 (DuckDuckGeese excluded) and EOData |
| seeds | 2020-2024 for the searches, `boruta` and `random`; the deterministic selectors once per dataset |
| budget | each dataset's H2/H3 fit budget, `results/h2h3-calibration/budgets.json` (2,000-12,000 fits); FS-SDS split evenly over k = 1..20 |
| per-run cap | 6 hours; 12 cells not run ([`h5h6_skip.txt`](h5h6_skip.txt), with reasons); any other run reaching 6 h is stopped and recorded as timed out |
| threads | one per numeric library (DEC-37) |
| primary metric | test AUF, K = 10 |
| criterion | H5: stock FASTENER has more wins than losses against every other algorithm (all features excepted), each significant by a two-sided Wilcoxon over the datasets both were run on, Holm-corrected across the pairs, alpha = 0.05. H6: the same for guided-swap FASTENER, stock FASTENER included |
| secondary | the full algorithm-by-algorithm matrix; results by cluster; cells without a result and why; fits and CPU per arm |

**How it runs.** One queue, the costliest datasets and arms first:

```
python experiments/run_queue.py --experiment H5H6-E01 \
    --datasets <the 39, by pilot cost> --methods <the 18, by pilot cost> \
    --budgets results/h2h3-calibration/budgets.json \
    --skip @experiments/h5h6_skip.txt --timeout-hours 6
```

Watched with `monitor.py --experiment H5H6-E01`.

**Expected cost.** About 410 CPU-hours from the pilot, so roughly 41 hours
at ten at a time; the longest predicted run is 5.2 hours.

### `H5H6-E02` -- H5 and H6 again, with a random forest in place of the tree

Registered 2026-10-26, after H5H6-E01 was reported and before any of this
experiment's runs started.

**Design.** H5H6-E01's grid, criteria and analysis, with one classifier
changed for every method ([DEC-41](DECISIONS.md#dec-41)):

| | |
|---|---|
| classifier | `RandomForestClassifier`, 100 trees, scikit-learn defaults otherwise, `random_state` 20200 (`run_sota.py --model rf`). It drives every search, fits Boruta's importances (through `TreeForBoruta`, at the protocol's 100 trees), RFE and the tree-importance ranker, and scores every subset. Arm labels are unchanged; the model is in every algorithm ID |
| arms | as H5H6-E01: the three FASTENER arms (clone `3be5697`), `nsga2`, `nsga2_sparse`, `nsgaii_miip`, `poss`, `fs_sds`, `boruta`, `random` and the eight filters, `all_features` a reference only |
| change to the searches | `nsga2`, `nsga2_sparse` and `nsgaii_miip` stop after 50 consecutive generations that score no new subset (`stopped_on="stagnation"`), as FS-SDS does after 50 barren sweeps. In H5H6-E01, 42 of the 47 timeouts were these searches spinning on cached subsets |
| datasets | as H5H6-E01: the 38 C1-C7 UCR/UEA sets and EOData |
| seeds | 2020-2024 for the searches, `boruta` and `random`; the deterministic selectors once per dataset |
| budget | H5H6-E01's fit budgets, committed as [`h2h3_budgets.json`](h2h3_budgets.json) (a copy of `results/h2h3-calibration/budgets.json`; 2,000-12,000 fits) |
| per-run cap | 6 hours, as H5H6-E01; the same 12 cells not run ([`h5h6_skip.txt`](h5h6_skip.txt)); any other run reaching 6 h is stopped and recorded as timed out |
| where | HPC Vega (allocation S26T11-01), one SLURM array task per job, 8 cores (the forest's trees and NSGAII-MIIP's MI matrix) and 8 GB each; 64 GB for the 12 jobs estimated to need more ([`h5h6e02_highmem.txt`](../hpc/vega/h5h6e02_highmem.txt)). At most about 880 node-hours if every job ran to the cap ([`hpc/vega/`](../hpc/vega/README.md)) |
| primary metric, criterion | as H5H6-E01: test AUF, K = 10; H5 and H6 as registered |
| secondary | the comparison with H5H6-E01, cell by cell: which conclusions survive the change of classifier (D2) |

**How it runs.** On a Vega login node, after `hpc/vega/setup.sh`:

```
bash hpc/vega/submit.sh hpc/vega/h5h6e02.env
```

`hpc/vega/h5h6e02.env` holds H5H6-E01's dataset and arm lists in its order,
its budgets, skip list and cap, and `--model rf`. A data job downloads,
verifies and converts the datasets, and the jobs start when it has succeeded.
Progress: `bash hpc/vega/status.sh hpc/vega/h5h6e02.env`. Results come home
with `hpc/vega/fetch.sh`.

**Expected cost.** No pilot was run. Forest fits timed on 2026-10-26 on 8
threads cost about 0.2 s each on the small sets (thread start-up dominates),
0.3-1.2 s on FordA and Crop, and 2.5-5.8 s on EOData, against 0.001-4.5 s for
the tree. A single forest fit can be cheaper than a tree fit on a wide
subset, since each split considers only the square root of the features. At
these rates the FASTENER runs take up to about 3 hours. The NSGA-II arms on
the largest datasets are the runs most likely to reach the cap.

**Changed during the run (2026-10-27, [DEC-42](DECISIONS.md#dec-42)).**
FASTENER cached every fitted forest and its jobs on mid-sized and large
datasets ran into their 8 GB; the model store is now bounded, with
identical fronts, scores and fits, and all 585 FASTENER jobs are rerun
under it. Runs of the other arms come from commits `5efd5a3` and
`f3eb230`, which differ only in how runs are recorded (cluster jobs no
longer write this ledger; `hpc_jobs.py ledger` does).

### `H5H6-E03` -- H5 and H6 again, with LightGBM as the one classifier

Registered 2026-10-28, after H5H6-E02 was reported and before any of this
experiment's runs started.

**Design.** H5H6-E01's grid, criteria and analysis, with LightGBM as the
classifier of every method ([DEC-44](DECISIONS.md#dec-44)); everything H5H6-E02
changed besides its classifier is kept:

| | |
|---|---|
| classifier | `LGBMClassifier`: LightGBM's defaults (100 trees, 31 leaves, learning rate 0.1) except the smallest leaf, `min_child_samples = min(20, max(1, n_train // 20))` -- a twentieth of the training rows, between 1 and LightGBM's default 20 -- with `deterministic=True`, `force_col_wise=True`, `random_state` 20200 (`run_sota.py --model lgbm`). It drives every search, fits Boruta's importances (through `TreeForBoruta`), RFE and the tree-importance ranker (split-count importances), and scores every subset. Each manifest records the dataset's leaf size |
| arms | as H5H6-E01 and H5H6-E02: the three FASTENER arms (clone `3be5697`, bounded model store, DEC-42), `nsga2`, `nsga2_sparse`, `nsgaii_miip` (stagnation stop, DEC-41), `poss`, `fs_sds`, `boruta`, `random` and the eight filters, `all_features` a reference only |
| datasets, seeds, budgets | as H5H6-E01: the 38 C1-C7 UCR/UEA sets and EOData; seeds 2020-2024 for the searches, `boruta` and `random`, the deterministic selectors once; the fit budgets of [`h2h3_budgets.json`](h2h3_budgets.json) |
| per-run cap | 6 hours; the same 12 cells not run ([`h5h6_skip.txt`](h5h6_skip.txt)) |
| where | HPC Vega (allocation S26T11-01), one SLURM array task per job, 8 cores and 8 GB each, 64 GB for the 12 jobs of [`h5h6e02_highmem.txt`](../hpc/vega/h5h6e02_highmem.txt) ([`hpc/vega/h5h6e03.env`](../hpc/vega/h5h6e03.env)) |
| provenance | each run reads its commit from `.git` (the compute nodes have no git) and counts as clean only if it is the commit `submit.sh` checked clean on the login node ([DEC-43](DECISIONS.md#dec-43)) |
| primary metric, criterion | as H5H6-E01: test AUF, K = 10; H5 and H6 as registered |
| secondary | the comparison with H5H6-E01 (tree) and H5H6-E02 (forest), cell by cell: which conclusions hold under a boosted learner (D2) |

**How it runs.** On Vega, after `git pull` and `bash hpc/vega/setup.sh` (the
environment now includes LightGBM):

```
bash hpc/vega/submit.sh hpc/vega/h5h6e03.env
```

The data are already on Vega from H5H6-E02 (`SKIP_DATA=1` may be used).
Results come home over ssh with `hpc/vega/fetch.sh`.

**Expected cost.** LightGBM fits timed on 2026-10-28 on 8 threads: 7-58 ms on
the small sets, 0.1 s on FordA, 0.7 s on ElectricDevices and 3.3 s on EOData,
against 0.2 s, 0.3-1.2 s and 2.5-5.8 s for the forest. Models are at most
8 MB (EOData). About H5H6-E02's 967 job-hours or fewer; at most about 880
node-hours of billing if every job ran to the cap.

### `H7-E01` -- H7: the H5/H6 grids' searches at 10% and 25% of their budget

Registered 2026-10-28, before any score at an intermediate checkpoint was
computed or read. The end-of-budget scores of the three grids had been read
(H5H6-E01 to -E03).

**Design.** An analysis of existing runs; no new search runs.

| | |
|---|---|
| runs read | every finished run (the reports' `tables/run_keys.csv`) of `fastener`, `nsga2_sparse` and `poss` -- H7's arms -- and of `fastener_swap_guided` and `fastener_swap_random`, reported beside them, in H5H6-E01 (decision tree), H5H6-E02 (random forest) and H5H6-E03 (LightGBM): the same cells the end-of-budget tests used. Timed-out runs are not read, as there |
| checkpoints | 10% and 25% of each run's fit budget (H7's criterion); 50% and 100% reported beside them |
| procedure | `experiments/h7_checkpoints.py`: per run and checkpoint, the last front the trajectory stored at or below that many fits (a run that stopped earlier contributes its final front); every entry of at most 10 features refitted with the grid's classifier on the training split and scored on validation and once on test by `protocol.evaluate_subset`; AUF (K = 10) by `make_report.opt_curve`, the reports' own function. One provenance run per grid and dataset (117) |
| ties | the trajectory keeps the first subset found at a validation score, the final records of POSS and sparse NSGA-II sometimes a later one with the same score. At 100% the procedure therefore reproduces every FASTENER run's end-of-budget AUF exactly and POSS's and sparse NSGA-II's up to that choice: in a dry run on four datasets (checkpoint 100% only), 274 of 289 runs matched and all 15 others had identical validation curves. All checkpoints are read the same way, from the trajectory |
| left out | FS-SDS, which HYPOTHESES.md reports beside H7: it runs k = 1..20 one after another, so a share of its total fits has seen only the first k's |
| criterion | as registered in HYPOTHESES.md, H7: per classifier, FASTENER against sparse NSGA-II and POSS at 10% and 25%, datasets as the unit, Wilcoxon with Holm over the four; supported if all four hold under at least two of the three classifiers |
| where | this workstation, 12 processes; the datasets and the grids' run folders are on disk |

### `H10-E01` -- H10: FASTENER seeded from mRMR and from Boruta, on H5H6-E03's grid

Registered 2026-10-29, before any of its runs.

**Design.** Two new arms on H5H6-E03's grid and setup; everything they are
compared with comes from H5H6-E03.

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener_seed_mrmr` (primary) and `fastener_seed_boruta` (secondary): stock FASTENER (clone `3be5697`, fixed mating, top genes kept, no swap) whose initial population is the filter's top-k subsets for k = 1..10 instead of five random single features (`run_sota.py --fastener-arm seed_mrmr / seed_boruta`). mRMR as `sota_baselines.rank_mrmr`, deterministic, computed once per run; Boruta as H5H6-E03's (`sota_baselines.boruta_ranking`, 100 iterations, LightGBM importances), with the run's seed |
| budget | the dataset's H2/H3 fit budget per run, as H5H6-E03. The ten seed subsets are scored as fits of the run; Boruta's own fits (one per iteration, recorded as `seed_fits`) are subtracted from the run's FASTENER budget, so seed and search together spend exactly the budget. mRMR fits no model; its CPU and wall time are recorded (`seed_cpu_seconds`, `seed_seconds`) |
| classifier, data, cap | as H5H6-E03 (DEC-44): LightGBM, 39 datasets, seeds 2020-2024, 6 hours per run; no cell skipped |
| compared with | H5H6-E03's `fastener` and `mrmr` runs (commit `fbbecfe`). The code those arms run is unchanged since; the new arms differ from `fastener` only in the initial population and the budget charged for the seed |
| criterion | HYPOTHESES.md, H10, for `fastener_seed_mrmr`: (a) against mRMR alone, AUF (K = 10) per dataset, more wins than losses and a two-sided Wilcoxon p < 0.05; (b) against stock FASTENER at the full budget, the one-sided 95% lower bound of the mean per-dataset difference above -0.005 (paired bootstrap over datasets, 10,000 resamples, seed 20200); (c) against stock FASTENER at 10% and 25% of the budget, read as in H7-E01 (`h7_checkpoints.py`'s procedure), each with more wins than losses and a two-sided Wilcoxon test significant after Holm over the two. Supported if (a), (b) and (c) hold. `fastener_seed_boruta` is reported against the same three, as secondary |
| checkpoints for a seeded run | the seed's subsets are among the run's first fits, so a checkpoint of c x budget reads the front at c x budget fits; for the Boruta seed, whose own fits precede FASTENER's and are not in its trajectory, at c x budget - seed_fits (the front before any FASTENER fit when that is negative) |
| where | HPC Vega, `hpc/vega/h10e01.env`: 390 jobs (2 arms x 39 datasets x 5 seeds), 8 cores and 8 GB each, 64 GB for the Boruta-seeded arm on PEMS-SF, MotorImagery and EigenWorms (`h10e01_highmem.txt`) |
| expected cost | about H5H6-E03's FASTENER cost per arm (105-124 job-hours) plus the seed: about 250 job-hours; longest expected run about 5 h (PhonemeSpectra, Boruta seed) |

### `H10-E02` -- H10 under the random forest, on H5H6-E02's grid

Registered 2026-10-29, before any H10 result had been fetched or read
([DEC-46](DECISIONS.md#dec-46)).

**Design.** H10-E01's design with H5H6-E02 as the base grid:

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener_seed_mrmr` (primary), `fastener_seed_boruta` (secondary), exactly as in H10-E01 (Boruta's importances from the random forest, as in H5H6-E02) |
| budget, data, seeds, cap | as H5H6-E02: each dataset's H2/H3 fit budget ([`h2h3_budgets.json`](h2h3_budgets.json)), the 39 datasets, seeds 2020-2024, 6 hours per run, 8 cores; no cell skipped |
| compared with | H5H6-E02's `fastener` and `mrmr` runs; stock FASTENER's checkpoints from H7-E01 (its random-forest grid) |
| criterion | H10-E01's (a), (b), (c), unchanged, under the random forest |
| where | HPC Vega, `hpc/vega/h10e02.env`: 390 jobs, 8 cores and 8 GB each (H5H6-E02 ran Boruta in 8 GB) |
| expected cost | about twice H5H6-E02's stock-FASTENER search time (108 h) plus Boruta's (4 h): about 220 job-hours; longest run under 2 h |

### `H10-E03` -- H10 under the decision tree, on H5H6-E01's grid

Registered 2026-10-29, with H10-E02 ([DEC-46](DECISIONS.md#dec-46)).

**Design.** H10-E01's design with H5H6-E01 as the base grid:

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | as H10-E01, under the protocol decision tree |
| budget, data, seeds, cap | as H5H6-E01: the same budgets, 39 datasets, seeds 2020-2024 and 6-hour cap; one core per run and one thread per numeric library (DEC-37); no cell skipped |
| compared with | H5H6-E01's `fastener` and `mrmr` runs; stock FASTENER's checkpoints from H7-E01 (its tree grid) |
| criterion | H10-E01's (a), (b), (c), unchanged, under the decision tree |
| environment | H5H6-E01 ran on the workstation with scikit-learn 1.9.0, Vega's environment has 1.9.1 (requirements.txt is not pinned); the PI judged the two equivalent for the tree, so the seeded runs are compared with H5H6-E01's directly, without a reproduction run |
| where | HPC Vega, `hpc/vega/h10e03.env`: 390 jobs, 1 core and 8 GB each. Timings are not comparable with H5H6-E01's |
| expected cost | about twice H5H6-E01's stock-FASTENER search time (14 h) plus Boruta's (9 h): about 40-50 single-core hours; longest run under 1 h |

### `H11-E01` -- H11 (post hoc): H10's criterion for the Boruta seed

Registered 2026-10-29, **after** H10-E01 to -E03's results were read
([DEC-48](DECISIONS.md#dec-48)).

**Design.** No runs of its own.

| | |
|---|---|
| data | H10-E01 to -E03's `fastener_seed_boruta` runs and checkpoints; H5H6-E01 to -E03's `fastener` and `boruta` runs; stock FASTENER's checkpoints from H7-E01 |
| criterion | H10's (a), (b), (c), unchanged, for `fastener_seed_boruta`, per classifier; verdict under LightGBM (H10-E01) |
| computation | `h10_report.py`, already run for H10-E01 to -E03 (`tables/criterion.csv`, `verdict.json` in each report); nothing recomputed |
| status of the result | post hoc: the claim was formed from these results |

### `H8-E01` -- H8: an LLM relevance prior for FASTENER, three Anthropic models

Registered 2026-10-30, before any prior was made and before any run
([DEC-49](DECISIONS.md#dec-49)).

**Design.**

| | |
|---|---|
| arms (14) | `fastener` (stock: five random singles, mutual information); `fastener_seed_mrmr` (H10's data-only seed, the control a prior from the data alone gives); per model m in opus, sonnet, haiku: `fastener_llm_m` (the prior guiding mating, mutation and the initial singles, lambda 0.5), `fastener_llm_m_scrambled` (the same with the scrambled prior), `fastener_llm_m_proposer` (the prior plus the online proposer); with Opus: `fastener_llm_opus_mating`, `_mutation`, `_initial` (the prior on one operator; the others as stock) |
| models | `anthropic/claude-opus-5.5`, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`, `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5` on OpenRouter, temperature 0, seed 20200 |
| priors | one per dataset and model, named and scrambled (96 files, `experiments/priors/<dataset>__<model>[__scrambled].json`), made with `llm_guidance.py prior` before any run and committed with the call log (`priors/calls.jsonl`); a run reads its prior file and records its SHA256 |
| proposer | every 5 generations, up to 5 subsets of at most 10 features, from the front with feature names; evaluated from the run's fit budget; each run's calls logged in its folder |
| datasets | the 16 of `DATASETS_USED.md`, "H8: datasets with named features"; the 50,000-row cap |
| classifier, budget, seeds, cap | LightGBM; 1,000 fits per run; seeds 2020-2024; 6 hours per run |
| runs | 14 arms x 16 datasets x 5 seeds = 1,120; on HPC Vega, 8 cores per run (`hpc/vega/h8e01.env`; [DEC-50](DECISIONS.md#dec-50), revised 2026-10-30 before any result: registered first for the workstation) |
| primary metric | fits to a common target per pair of arms, dataset and seed: the target is the lower of the two runs' final validation AUF (K = 10), read from their trajectories; a run's value is the fits at its first snapshot at or above it |
| criterion | per model: (i) `fastener_llm_m` vs `fastener`, (ii) `fastener_llm_m` vs `fastener_llm_m_scrambled`; effect per dataset the mean over seeds of log2(opponent's fits / LLM arm's fits); each part needs more positive datasets than negative and a two-sided Wilcoxon p < 0.05 after Holm over the three models; H8 supported if (i) and (ii) hold for at least one model |
| beside it | the proposer arm against the prior arm and against stock; the ablations against stock and against `fastener_llm_opus`; `fastener_seed_mrmr` against stock and the LLM arms; end-of-budget test AUF (K = 10) of every arm; tokens, cost and wall time of the calls; prior against mutual information (Spearman) |

### `H8-E02` -- H8: the strict (blind) control for H8-E01's part (ii)

Registered 2026-10-30, before its priors were made and before any H8-E01
result was read ([DEC-51](DECISIONS.md#dec-51)).

**Design.** H8-E01's setup with three more arms.

| | |
|---|---|
| arms | `fastener_llm_<m>_blind` for m in opus, sonnet, haiku: H8-E01's prior arm (all three operators, lambda 0.5) with the blind prior |
| blind prior | `llm_guidance.LLMFeaturePrior(blind=True)`: the features in a shuffled order (`numpy.random.RandomState(20200).permutation`), each shown as `feature_<position>`; the task and class names, no dataset description, no feature descriptions; scores mapped back to the columns; one per dataset and model (48 files `priors/<dataset>__<model>__blind.json`, with the permutation), made with a spending cap |
| datasets, classifier, budget, seeds, cap | as H8-E01: 16 datasets, LightGBM, 1,000 fits, seeds 2020-2024, 6 hours |
| runs | 3 x 16 x 5 = 240 on HPC Vega, `hpc/vega/h8e02.env`; no model calls during the runs |
| criterion | H8's part (ii) for each model: H8-E01's `fastener_llm_<m>` against this experiment's `fastener_llm_<m>_blind`, as registered (fits to the common target, Holm over the three models); read with H8-E01 in one report |

### `H8-E03` -- H8 with nine non-Anthropic models

Registered 2026-10-30, after H8-E01 was reported and before any H8-E03 prior
was made or run started ([DEC-52](DECISIONS.md#dec-52)).

**Design.** H8-E01's, with H8-E02's blind control per model.

| | |
|---|---|
| models | `meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct`, `google/gemma-3-4b-it`, `google/gemma-3-27b-it`, `mistralai/ministral-8b-2512`, `qwen/qwen3-14b`, `microsoft/phi-4`, `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`, `openai/gpt-6-luna`, `google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite`; OpenRouter, temperature 0, seed 20200 |
| arms (41) | per model m: `fastener_llm_m` (prior on mating, mutation and the start, lambda 0.5), `fastener_llm_m_scrambled`, `fastener_llm_m_blind`, `fastener_llm_m_proposer` (every 5 generations, up to 5 subsets, at most $0.015 of calls per run); `fastener`, `fastener_seed_mrmr`; `fastener_llm_llama70b_mating`, `_mutation`, `_initial` |
| priors | 27 per dataset (9 models x named, scrambled, blind), made with `llm_guidance.py batch --max-cost` before any run and committed with the call log; tolerant parsing (`parse_scores`) and one retry for a chunk missing more than 20% of its features |
| datasets, classifier, budget, seeds, cap | as H8-E01: 16 datasets, LightGBM, 1,000 fits, seeds 2020-2024, 6 hours |
| runs | 41 x 16 x 5 = 3280, on HPC Vega (`hpc/vega/h8e03.env`) |
| criterion | H8's, per model, Holm over the nine: (i) `fastener_llm_m` vs `fastener`, (ii) vs `fastener_llm_m_blind`; fits to the common target, log2 ratio per dataset, more positive than negative and Wilcoxon p < 0.05. Read beside H8-E01/-E02 |
| beside it | as H8-E01: scrambled vs named and vs blind, proposer vs prior and vs stock, ablations, mRMR seed, end-of-budget test AUF, spend, prior agreement |
| cost | priors about $0.50 (cap $1.65); proposer at most $10.80 (720 runs x $0.015) |
